# Blender-via-MCP tutorials

A small, growing set of Blender tutorials that the studio runs **through the bench's MCP socket** rather than by hand in the viewport. The point of this folder isn't "here's how to model a thing in Blender"; it's "here's a Python script you can fire at a running Blender 5.1 over the MCP lattice and get a real `.glb` out the other side, repeatably."

Each tutorial is paired with a tutorial page under `/tutorials/blender-mcp-<slug>` so the script reads in context — what it builds, how the studio uses it, what to swap when adapting it for the sculpture gallery.

## Honest status — first pass

**The MCP bridge was not reachable from the agent that authored this pass.** Blender's socket on `127.0.0.1:9876` was listening — confirmed via `netstat` — but the harness running this agent denies arbitrary outbound TCP from its Bash tool, so the scripts in this pass have **not** been round-tripped end-to-end from inside the agent. Every tutorial entry carries a header banner saying so.

What that means concretely:

- The `.py` scripts in each tutorial entry are real — pulled verbatim from the cited OSS source, then adapted to the studio's export conventions (Y-up, applied transforms, Draco 6, WebP textures, flat-shaded normals).
- The expected output GLBs (`public/models/blender-tutorials/<slug>/<mesh>.glb`) and the render thumbnails (`thumb.png`) **have not been generated** in this pass. The `public/models/blender-tutorials/` tree is intentionally empty — better an empty directory than a fabricated artefact.
- The follow-up pass — run from a harness that can speak to `127.0.0.1:9876` — is supposed to execute each script, write the GLBs and thumbs, and flip the "not yet executed" banners off.

If you're reading this from such a harness, the run-order is at the foot of this doc.

## The MCP setup

Two skills carry the full setup:

- `blender-mcp` — the socket protocol, the FastMCP SSE server (`D:\The_Hangar\mcp\blender_mcp_sse.py`), the meshgen Blender add-on (Hyper3D path B), thread-safety constraints.
- `blender-pipelines` — the four production-grade studio pipelines on Sovereign-PC (fabrication chain, aura trails, Hunyuan, Looking Glass quilt). Carries the `bld_remote_mcp` quirks, the canonical addon inventory, the timer-pattern for blocking render ops.

Short version: open Blender 5.1, run `D:\The_Hangar\mcp\blender_mcp_sse.py` from the Scripting tab, confirm port `9876` is listening, send newline-delimited JSON of the form `{"type": "execute_code", "params": {"code": "<script>"}}` over a TCP socket.

Bench requirements:

- Blender 5.1.0 (`D:\The_Hangar\engines\blender5\blender.exe`)
- `bld_remote_mcp` add-on registered, server started
- For tutorials touching glTF export: nothing extra — the built-in exporter handles Draco + WebP
- For Tutorial 02 (parametric torus): `scipy` is **not** required; the script is pure `math`
- The render thumbnail step uses `BLENDER_EEVEE` (not `BLENDER_EEVEE_NEXT` — that name was 4.x only, see `blender-pipelines` gotcha 1)

## The catalogue

Four tutorials in this pass, all from MIT-licensed OSS sources, all adapted to the studio's conventions.

| Slug | Source URL | Licence | What it builds | Target GLB size |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `blender-mcp-faceted-sphere` | [njanakiev/blender-scripting/simple_sphere.py](https://github.com/njanakiev/blender-scripting/blob/master/scripts/simple_sphere.py) | MIT | A flat-shaded icosphere with the studio's facet conventions baked in — the canonical "hello world" for the WebXR exporter | < 50 KB |
| `blender-mcp-parametric-torus` | [njanakiev/blender-scripting/parametric_torus.py](https://github.com/njanakiev/blender-scripting/blob/master/scripts/parametric_torus.py) | MIT | A torus mesh built from a `(u, v)` surface parameterisation rather than the primitive operator — useful when you want exact control over segment counts | < 200 KB |
| `blender-mcp-tetra-fractal` | [njanakiev/blender-scripting/tetrahedron_fractal.py](https://github.com/njanakiev/blender-scripting/blob/master/scripts/tetrahedron_fractal.py) | MIT | A recursive Sierpinski-style tetrahedron fractal; depth-4 produces a richly-faceted mesh that decimates well | < 2 MB at level 4 |
| `blender-mcp-cube-walker` | [aaronjolson/Blender-Python-Procedural-Level-Generation/random_walk_via_cube_placement.py](https://github.com/aaronjolson/Blender-Python-Procedural-Level-Generation/blob/master/Blender_2_8/random_walk_via_cube_placement.py) | MIT | A random-walk maze of joined-and-hollowed cubes — the simplest possible procedural environment generator, useful for splat-walker placeholder geometry | < 1 MB at 1000 iterations |

GLB size budgets are targets, not measurements — see the "honest status" note above.

## Execution methodology (how to reproduce)

For each tutorial:

1. Open Blender 5.1, switch to the Scripting workspace, open and run `D:\The_Hangar\mcp\blender_mcp_sse.py`. Confirm `Server running on 127.0.0.1:9876` in the system console.
2. From a terminal that can reach `127.0.0.1:9876`, send the tutorial's `.py` payload as the `code` field of `{"type": "execute_code", "params": {"code": "..."}}` — newline-delimited JSON over a raw TCP socket, **not** HTTP. (See `blender-pipelines` for why HTTP is wrong here.)
3. Each script ends by calling `bpy.ops.export_scene.gltf(...)` with the canonical Holoflow flags and writing to `public/models/blender-tutorials/<slug>/<mesh>.glb`.
4. Each script then optionally calls `bpy.ops.render.render(write_still=True)` with the output set to `public/models/blender-tutorials/<slug>/thumb.png` at 512×512.
5. Heavy scripts (Tetra Fractal at depth ≥ 5, Cube Walker at ≥ 5000 iterations) take more than 4 seconds and **will** drop the MCP response — the script writes to a sidecar `.done` marker file the caller polls for, per the `blender-pipelines` timer pattern.

The 4-second timeout rule from `blender-pipelines` is the single most important thing to understand here. Anything that won't return inside that window needs the marker-file pattern, not the response payload.

## What failed in this pass — for the next agent

- **Outbound TCP from this agent's harness was denied.** Every attempt to run `python /tmp/blender_probe.py` or `python -c "import socket; ..."` was refused by the sandbox even though the MCP socket was confirmed listening. This is a harness-policy issue, not a Blender or MCP problem.
- **`git push` to `claude/skeleton-build` was not attempted.** The agent's checkout is on `holoflow-commerce`; the instructions called for a push to `claude/skeleton-build`. The local checkout state is preserved; the next pass — or a human operator — should review the diff, switch to the correct branch (or cherry-pick), and push.
- **`pnpm typecheck` was not attempted from this agent** for the same reason — the harness denied long-running builds. The tutorial entries follow the patterns of the existing `blender-addon-*` entries verbatim, so the type surface is the same; nothing in this pass introduces a new shape.

## Future passes

- Run each script end-to-end via the MCP, attach real GLBs + thumbs, flip the banners off.
- Add a fifth tutorial: **VRM avatar export end-to-end** — load a VRM via the saturday06 add-on, retarget to a Mixamo rig, export as a Holoflow-conventions GLB. (Cross-references `blender-addon-vrm-format` and `blender-addon-mixamo-rig`.)
- Add a sixth tutorial: **glTF animation bake** — author a short turntable in Blender, bake at 30 fps, export with the studio's animation conventions, render preview frames for the tutorial page.
- Wire the GLBs into the sculpture gallery's catalogue so each tutorial's outcome appears as a walkable piece at `/atelier/sculpture-gallery/blender-mcp-<slug>`.
- Replace the screenshot-thumb step with a real render: enable `BLENDER_EEVEE`, set a three-point sun rig, set the output to `public/models/blender-tutorials/<slug>/thumb.png` at 512×512 with `render.resolution_percentage = 100`.

## 2026-05-19 — library series (public/library/ pipeline)

The `public/library/` directory introduces a parallel authoring pipeline to the
MCP-socket tutorials above. Where the MCP series fires scripts over the bench's
TCP socket, the library series ships headless-runnable blueprints alongside the
source `.blend` and export artefacts. Every library entry carries its own
`record.py` for viewport animation and `SCREEN-RECORDING-NOTES.md` for the OBS
screen-capture session.

### faceted-gemstone-geonodes

| Field | Value |
|-------|-------|
| **Tutorial slug** | `blender-tutorial-faceted-gemstone-geonodes` |
| **Tutorial URL** | `/tutorials/blender-tutorial-faceted-gemstone-geonodes` |
| **Library path** | `public/library/blends/procedural/faceted-gemstone-geonodes/` |
| **Blender** | 5.1 |
| **Technique** | UV sphere (8 seg × 6 rings) → bmesh crown/girdle/pavilion zones → flat shading → Principled BSDF IOR 2.42 → GN parametric sliders via `nt.interface.new_socket()` → Draco GLB |
| **Artefacts** | `gemstone.blend`, `gemstone.glb`, `viewport.mp4`, `screen.mp4` |
| **Source 1** | Blender Manual — GN Convex Hull (Blender Foundation, CC-BY-4.0) |
| **Source 2** | geometry-script by Carson Katri (MIT) |
| **Date** | 2026-05-19 |

**Notes:**
- First entry in `public/library/` — establishes the full directory shape
  (`blueprint.py`, `record.py`, `README.md`, `SCREEN-RECORDING-NOTES.md`,
  `.expected-artefacts.json`) and the `MANIFEST.md` row format.
- Demonstrates Blender 5.x GN interface API (`nt.interface.new_socket`)
  replacing the removed 3.x `nt.inputs.new()` / `nt.outputs.new()`.
- UV sphere ring topology (8×6) maps cleanly to gem zones — each ring index
  is one latitude band, making bmesh zone selection deterministic.
- Headless run: `blender --background --python blueprint.py`

| 2026-05-30 | blender-tutorial-gn-mesh-island-per-island-colour | GN Mesh Island — Per-Island Colour, Random Offset, and Separation | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-mesh-island-per-island-colour/ | GeometryNodeInputMeshIsland outputs Island Index (INT) that adapts to the active domain context — no CaptureAttribute required; at FACE domain every face in a connected component shares the same index; at POINT domain all vertices of a tile share the index; RandomValue(FLOAT_VECTOR, ID=IslandIndex, Seed=42) gives constant-per-island random colour because all elements of an island share the same IslandIndex — identical ID → identical random output; RandomValue(FLOAT, ID=IslandIndex, Seed=99) for per-island Z lift with a separate seed to ensure colour/lift are uncorrelated; GAP > 0 between tiles is mandatory — GAP == 0 risks vertex merging and incorrect island count; StoreNamedAttribute(domain='FACE', data_type='FLOAT_COLOR') for island_colour; StoreNamedAttribute(domain='FACE', data_type='INT') for island_index — this materialised INT is the prerequisite for downstream SeparateGeometry island selection (NamedAttribute + Compare EQUAL + SeparateGeometry); Island Count (second output of MeshIsland) is a singular INT useful for normalising Island Index to 0..1 for ColorRamp gradient palettes; export_attributes=True required — island_colour appears as _ISLAND_COLOUR in glTF binary accessor, island_index as _ISLAND_INDEX; Lift Multiplier group socket keyframed 0→1→0 in record.py for mosaic-reveal viewport animation; outside sources: Blender Manual Mesh Island Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-02 | blender-tutorial-gn-sample-index-echo-grid | GN Sample Index — Echo-Grid Interference Deformation | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-sample-index-echo-grid/ | GeometryNodeSampleIndex data_type='FLOAT' domain='POINT': inputs[0]=Geometry (explicit lookup target), inputs[1]=Value (field evaluated on that geometry at the given index), inputs[2]=Index (INT), inputs[3]=Clamp (BOOLEAN=False); reads src_z at index (i + ROW_OFFSET) % TOTAL — the vertex one full grid row ahead in +Y; GeometryNodeStoreNamedAttribute must precede any SetPosition: position field nodes and NamedAttribute reads inside the SetPosition sub-tree still reference the pre-deformation flat-grid coordinates because GN evaluates fields lazily; final_Z = own_src_z + echo_src_z × ECHO_SCALE — linear superposition of two noise phases creates constructive peaks and destructive troughs; ShaderNodeMath(ADD) on Index (INT→FLOAT auto-cast) + Row Offset INT socket, then ShaderNodeMath(MODULO) with TOTAL_POINTS float literal — output float auto-truncated to INT at SampleIndex.Index socket; echo_scale and row_offset exposed as live group sockets (NodeSocketFloat + NodeSocketInt); ShaderNodeAttribute(attribute_type='GEOMETRY', attribute_name='src_z') reads frozen float in material for gradient visualisation; export_apply=True mandatory — vertex positions in GLB reflect the full interference deformation; outside sources: Blender Manual Sample Index Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-02 | blender-tutorial-gn-blur-attribute-heat-diffusion | GN Blur Attribute — Heat Diffusion Dome | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-blur-attribute-heat-diffusion/ | GeometryNodeBlurAttribute data_type='FLOAT': inputs[0]=Value(field), inputs[1]=Iterations(INT), inputs[2]=Weight(FLOAT field, default=1.0); it is a FIELD node — no Geometry input, domain context is set by the consuming StoreNamedAttribute; two-store pattern required: StoreNamedAttribute("heat") materialises the binary seed BEFORE BlurAttribute reads it via NamedAttribute("heat") — without this, Blur re-evaluates the raw field per iteration and never propagates beyond the seed boundary; Weight socket: FunctionNodeCompare(ABS(Z) < EQUATOR_WIDTH) → GeometryNodeSwitch(input_type='FLOAT', False=1.0, True=GroupInput.Resistance) — values below 1.0 slow propagation at that element, creating a discrete thermal-insulation band; on a UV sphere (32 rings) Iterations≈12 reaches the equator; convergence depends on mesh density, not geometry scale; POINT domain gives per-vertex smooth interpolation; FACE domain gives faceted constant-per-face result; EDGE domain spreads along edge-adjacency graph; export_attributes=True required to write heat_blurred as _HEAT_BLURRED custom glTF vertex accessor; ShaderNodeAttribute(attribute_type='GEOMETRY', attribute_name='heat_blurred').outputs["Fac"] for scalar read in material; outside sources: Blender Manual Blur Attribute CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-03 | blender-tutorial-gn-sample-nearest-surface-mesh-conform | GN Sample Nearest Surface — Mesh-Conform Tile Armour | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-sample-nearest-surface-mesh-conform/ | GeometryNodeSampleNearestSurface data_type=FLOAT_VECTOR or FLOAT: inputs[0]=Mesh (target geometry socket), inputs[1]=Value (field evaluated ON THE TARGET mesh domains), inputs[2]=Sample Position (evaluated on current geometry = query positions), inputs[3]=Group ID (INT, 4.1+); outputs[0]=Value (sampled attribute), outputs[1]=Is Valid (BOOLEAN); two-context evaluation: InputPosition connected to Value socket evaluates on the target mesh vertex positions; InputPosition connected to Sample Position evaluates on scatter-point positions in the current geometry — identical node type, completely different evaluation context; three-SNS pattern: SNS#1 data_type=FLOAT_VECTOR Value=InputPosition → snapped surface position; SNS#2 data_type=FLOAT_VECTOR Value=InputNormal → surface normal for tile alignment; SNS#3 data_type=FLOAT Value=NamedAttribute("latitude") → colour driver; all three share one InputPosition node for Sample Position — they query the same nearest surface point, guaranteeing consistent snapped position + normal + attribute; FunctionNodeAlignEulerToVector axis=Z pivot_axis=AUTO: maps tile local +Z to surface outward normal; connects directly to Instance On Points Rotation — field node, no Realize Instances needed; point-before-instance discipline: Set Position + Store Named Attribute + Align Euler on scatter POINTS before Instance On Points; tile_lat stored domain=POINT propagates as per-instance uniform to shader — all vertices of one tile read same float, giving per-tile colour not per-vertex colour; contrast with Geometry Proximity (returns distance and nearest position, cannot sample arbitrary attributes); SCATTER_RADIUS > CONFORM_RADIUS is critical for convex targets — query points must start outside surface for Is_Valid = True; retargeting: swap Object socket + ensure target has "latitude" POINT attribute; export_attributes=True required — tile_lat exports as _TILE_LAT glTF custom accessor; outside sources: Sample Nearest Surface Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) + blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) |

outputs['Min'] and outputs['Max'] are global scalar constants — uniform across the whole geometry, recalculated every modifier evaluation; critical distinction: AttributeStatistic COLLAPSES a field to scalars, it does NOT store or transfer an attribute; wire outputs['Min']→MapRange.inputs[1] and outputs['Max']→MapRange.inputs[2] for a self-calibrating normaliser that works regardless of mesh scale or displacement; GeometryNodeInputFaceArea is a FACE-domain field — evaluates to 0 at any other domain unless wrapped in GeometryNodeFieldOnDomain; export_attributes=True required — face_heat→_FACE_HEAT and vertex_heat→_VERTEX_HEAT in GLB primitive attribute dict; ShaderNodeAttribute(attribute_type='GEOMETRY', attribute_name='face_heat').outputs['Color'] reads face-constant RGBA in Principled BSDF; | 2026-06-03 | blender-tutorial-gn-points-to-volume-organic-coral | GN Points to Volume + Volume to Mesh — Procedural Organic Coral Blob | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-points-to-volume-organic-coral/ | GeometryNodePointsToVolume resolution_mode='VOXEL_SIZE': inputs[0]=Points(Geometry), inputs['Voxel Size']=world-space voxel edge length, inputs['Radius']=Gaussian kernel half-width; Volume is a distinct geometry type — mesh nodes cannot operate on a Volume socket; GeometryNodeVolumeToMesh resolution_mode='GRID': inherits incoming VDB voxel resolution without resampling; GRID is the correct choice when PointsToVolume defines resolution — VOXEL_SIZE on VolumeToMesh resamples and loses detail; inputs[1]=Threshold(FLOAT) isosurface density level, inputs[2]=Adaptivity(FLOAT 0..1) marching-cubes decimation; 3× rule: POINT_RADIUS >= VOXEL_SIZE × 3 or staircase artefacts appear; kernel overlap density: ρ_midpoint ≈ 2×exp(-d²/(2r²)); THRESHOLD controls erosion from tips inward; Adaptivity 0.4–0.5 → 60–75% triangle reduction; marching cubes produces NO UV coordinates — use world-space procedural shading (ShaderNodeNewGeometry.Position → TexNoise) or bake to cage UV; SetShadeSmooth(domain='FACE', smooth=False) for faceted aesthetic; export_apply=True mandatory; outside sources: Points to Volume Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + Volume to Mesh Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) + OpenVDB Apache-2.0 (Academy Software Foundation) |

| 2026-06-03 | blender-tutorial-gn-fillet-curve-neon-sign | GN Fillet Curve — Procedural Rounded-Corner Neon Star Sign | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-fillet-curve-neon-sign/ | GeometryNodeFilletCurve mode='BEZIER' or 'POLY': BEZIER inserts smooth bezier-handled arcs at every spline kink; POLY subdivides with straight segments; BEZIER mode requires downstream GeometryNodeResampleCurve (mode='COUNT') because BEZIER arcs exist only in evaluated data — without resampling, CurveToMesh sweeps the original control-point polyline and the rounding is invisible; Count input controls arc segment count (4 = smooth for tube scale); Clamped=True prevents arcs exceeding half adjacent edge length, avoiding overlap at tight corners; hexagonal star (CurvePrimitiveStar Points=6, Outer=1.0, Inner=0.42) has 12 corners — 6 obtuse outer spikes, 6 acute inner valleys; inner valley side length ≈ 0.44 units so Fillet_Radius=0.22 rounds inner valleys to soft U-shapes while outer spikes stay sharp; CurvePrimitiveCircle Resolution=8 for octagonal tube cross-section — adequate silhouette, minimal triangle count; CurveToMesh Fill_Caps=True; SetShadeSmooth domain=FACE; emissive neon material: Mix Shader (Fac=0.6) Principled BSDF + Emission Strength=8.0 → EEVEE Bloom halo; group sockets: Fillet_Radius (0.001–1.0, default 0.22), Resample_Count (32–512, default 160), Tube_Radius (0.005–0.15, default 0.028); export_apply=True; GLB ~80 KB Draco level 6; outside sources: Fillet Curve Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-03 | blender-tutorial-shader-principled-transmission-iridescence | Principled BSDF v2 — Transmission + Iridescence: Holographic Gem Shader | blends/shading/shader-principled-transmission-iridescence/ | ShaderNodeBsdfPrincipled v2 socket-name renames from Blender 3.x→4.0 still valid in 5.1: Transmission→Transmission Weight, Clearcoat→Coat Weight, Clearcoat Roughness→Coat Roughness, Emission→Emission Color; new in 4.0: Iridescence Weight, Iridescence IOR, Iridescence Thickness; Transmission Weight != Alpha — Transmission routes light through a Fresnel dielectric model (Snell law, IOR-dependent), Alpha discards the fragment; Iridescence Thickness (nm) drives which wavelength constructively interferes — varies Wave.Fac (0-1) via ShaderNodeMapRange to physical nm range (270-680) → Principled BSDF Iridescence Thickness; driving Thickness sweeps spectrum; driving Weight only brightens/dims; Wave Texture BANDS+DIAGONAL+SIN breaks symmetry so iridescence reads as natural diffraction; ShaderNodeTexVoronoi feature=F2 distance=EUCLIDEAN — F2 returns distance to second nearest point, bright cell rings + dark interior analogous to crystal cleavage planes visible through Transmission window; ShaderNodeMix data_type=RGBA blend_type=MIX — 4.0+ unified node replacing deprecated ShaderNodeMixRGB; ShaderNodeBump Strength=0.22 Distance=0.002 — bump not displacement to preserve icosphere silhouette; EEVEE Next: scene.eevee.use_ssr=True + use_ssr_refraction=True required for Transmission; glTF-IO: Transmission Weight > 0 → KHR_materials_transmission auto-written; Iridescence Weight > 0 → KHR_materials_iridescence auto-written (iridescenceThicknessMinimum = Map Range To Min, Maximum = To Max); IOR → KHR_materials_ior; Three.js r152+ for iridescence; export_apply=True + use_selection=True + Draco 6; outside sources: Principled BSDF Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Foundation) + KHR_materials_iridescence Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-05 | blender-tutorial-gn-trim-curve-line-draw | GN Trim Curve — Animated Line-Draw Reveal on a Spiral Path | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-trim-curve-line-draw/ | GeometryNodeTrimCurve mode='FACTOR' or 'LENGTH': FACTOR clips [Start, End] on normalised [0,1] parameter — independent of arc length or scale, correct for draw-on animations; LENGTH clips at world-space metre values, correct for physical cable cutting; inputs[0]=Curve(Geometry), inputs[1]=Selection(BOOLEAN), inputs[2]=Start(FLOAT), inputs[3]=End(FLOAT); in FACTOR mode End=0.0 → zero-length result → CurveToMesh outputs no triangles (correct, not an error); GeometryNodeCurvePrimitiveSpiral: Resolution(INT) controls polyline density, Rotations(FLOAT) = full turns, Start/End Radius, Height=0.0 for flat Archimedean spiral; partial trim on spiral is immediately legible (radial growth inward → outward) vs trimmed circle (ambiguous arc); keyframing GN socket from Python requires socket identifier not name: de.identifier (e.g. 'Socket_2') captured at creation time; mod[draw_end_id]=0.0 + mod.keyframe_insert(data_path=f'["{draw_end_id}"]', frame=1); interpolation set to EASE_IN per keyframe_point for natural pen-stroke acceleration; scene.frame_set(ANIM_FRAMES) before gltf export operator so export_apply=True materialises full-spiral state; animation NOT embedded by default — Three.js drives reveal independently; Principled BSDF 4.0 unified emission: inputs['Emission Color'] + inputs['Emission Strength'] directly on BSDF, no Mix Shader needed; EEVEE Bloom Intensity=0.12 + Radius=5.0 for gold halo; outside sources: Trim Curve Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-05 | blender-tutorial-armature-ik-robot-arm | Armature Constraints + IK — Inverse Kinematics for a Stylised Robot Arm | blends/rigging/armature-ik-robot-arm/ | pose.bones["lower_arm"].constraints.new('INVERSE_KINEMATICS'): CCD (Cyclic Coordinate Descent) solver; chain_count=2 drives lower_arm + upper_arm; chain_count=0 drives all ancestors to root (expensive, unpredictable); IK_Target empty (display_type='SPHERE') = end-effector position goal; IK_Pole empty (display_type='ARROWS') positioned at (0, -0.8, ELBOW_Z) collapses elbow's infinite cone of valid solutions to one direction; ik.pole_angle=math.radians(-90) aligns bone local X to pole vector for zero-roll bones — each 90° increment rotates the elbow 90° (trial-and-error correction pattern: adjust in 90° steps); parent_type='ARMATURE' activates bone envelopes (NOT exported to glTF) vs parent_type='OBJECT' + Armature modifier + use_vertex_groups=True (writes JOINTS_0/WEIGHTS_0 accessors); hard vertex group split at ELBOW_Z (weight 1.0 / 0.0) correct for mechanical robot joint — no skin blending; organic character rigs need gradient weights across the joint; bmesh headless build: _cylinder() for tube segments, _sphere() for UV sphere joints, bm.to_mesh(me) then bm.free(); material index assignment by polygon.center.z proximity to joint Z positions; keyframe IK_Target orbit: ik_tgt.location.x = R × cos(2π×t), ik_tgt.location.z = ELBOW_Z + R×0.5×sin(2π×t), keyframe_insert('location', frame); IK solver re-evaluates every frame during playback — no baking needed for .blend, only needed to export solved animation to NLA strips; export_skins=True + export_animations=True + export_apply=True materialises posed mesh at scene.frame_set(15) for static GLB; JOINTS_0/WEIGHTS_0 spec: glTF 2.0 §Skins (KhronosGroup/glTF); outside sources: Blender Manual Inverse Kinematics CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-05 | blender-tutorial-gn-set-position-noise-displacement | GN Set Position + Noise Texture — Animated Blob Planet | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-set-position-noise-displacement/ | GeometryNodeSetPosition: inputs[0]=Geometry, inputs[1]=Selection(BOOLEAN), inputs[2]=Position(VECTOR—replaces absolutely), inputs[3]=Offset(VECTOR—adds delta); Offset preserves base mesh shape; Position destroys it for noise displacement — always use Offset for surface displacement; ShaderNodeTexNoise noise_dimensions='4D': inputs['Vector']=position field, inputs['W']=FLOAT fourth axis, inputs['Scale'/'Detail'/'Roughness'/'Distortion'/'Lacunarity']=FLOAT; outputs['Fac']=FLOAT scalar; W advances through a 4D noise field smoothly — correct for temporal animation without UV/topology change; animating Scale zooms in/out of same static field (wrong for breathing); SCRIPTED driver on modifier socket: mod.driver_add(f'["{w_id}"]'), drv.expression='frame*0.04167', var.targets[0].data_path='frame_current', id_type='SCENE'; w_id found via ng.interface.items_tree lookup by item.name and item.in_out; dual-scale layering: macro(Scale=1.5,Detail=2,Rough=0.40,Amp=0.28)×micro(Scale=9.0,Detail=5,Rough=0.65,Amp=0.055); centring per layer: (Fac-0.5)×2 maps [0,1]→[-1,1], then ×amplitude socket ∈[-AMP,+AMP] — symmetric breathing, not one-sided expansion; W seed decorrelation: Math ADD 3.0 and Math ADD 17.0 before each noise W input — layers breathe independently; freeze-before-deform: StoreNamedAttribute(data_type='FLOAT',domain='POINT',name='elevation_fac') upstream of SetPosition; elevation normalised: (combined+MAX_LIFT)/(2×MAX_LIFT) clamped [0,1]; material reads ShaderNodeAttribute(attribute_type='GEOMETRY',attribute_name='elevation_fac').outputs['Fac']→3-stop ColorRamp; ShaderNodeVectorMath SCALE: inputs[0]=Normal vector, inputs['Scale']=combined scalar — VectorMath SCALE exposes named Scale socket in Blender 4.0+; export_attributes=True required for elevation_fac → _ELEVATION_FAC glTF custom accessor; outside sources: Set Position Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-05 | blender-tutorial-grease-pencil-lineart-toon-outline | Grease Pencil Line Art — View-Dependent Ink Silhouette and Crease Outlines | blends/shading/grease-pencil-lineart-toon-outline/ | GP_LINEART modifier (GPv2 API, stable Blender 5.1): source_type="OBJECT" target object → GPENCIL object; operates on 3D evaluated geometry before any pixel draw — NOT a post-render image pass like Freestyle; contour edges = silhouette (front-face ↔ back-face boundary from camera POV); crease edges = adjacent faces whose dihedral angle exceeds crease_threshold radians; crease_threshold math.radians(30°) on subdivisions=2 icosphere captures all 480 internal edges (~68° dihedral per adjacent pair); raise to 65° = only 30 original icosahedron edges survive; Shade Flat required — smooth normals blend the dihedral across vertices and Line Art cannot detect creases on a mesh that looks smooth; GPv2 vs GPv3: GPENCIL object + GP_LINEART vs GREASEPENCIL + GREASE_PENCIL_LINEART (4.3+, API still settling in 5.x); create_gpencil_data(mat) adds grease_pencil sub-block to standard Material; gp_mat.grease_pencil.show_stroke=True, stroke_style="SOLID", color=black, show_fill=False; la.target_layer must match GP layer name exactly (case-sensitive); la.target_material = direct gp_mat reference; thickness=3 → 3 viewport pixels at 1080p; view-dependence: contour "rolls" around sphere surface each frame as camera orbits — strokes are NOT stored, rebuilt every evaluation; export_apply=True bakes Line Art strokes to static ribbon quad geometry in GLB; both mesh + GP ink shell export as sibling objects in same GLB file; GPv2 GLB export stable, GPv3 export may not be complete in 5.1; macro: tools/blender-addon/holoflow_macros/grease_pencil_lineart.py setup_lineart() helper; outside sources: Line Art Modifier Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-06 | blender-tutorial-gn-index-of-nearest-neural-web | GN Index of Nearest — Neural Web Connection Field on a UV Sphere | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-index-of-nearest-neural-web/ | GeometryNodeIndexOfNearest: inputs[0]=Position(VECTOR field, default=InputPosition), inputs[1]=Group ID(INT, default=0); outputs[0]=Index(INT — nearest OTHER element's index), outputs[1]=Has Neighbor(BOOLEAN); evaluates in the POINT domain of whatever geometry context is consuming the output — no explicit Geometry socket; key difference from GeometryNodeProximity: operates WITHIN the same point cloud (intra-cloud pairing), not cross-object; pair with GeometryNodeSampleIndex(data_type, domain='POINT', index=ion.outputs["Index"]) to fetch any stored attribute at the neighbour's index — identical to Sample Index usage in the Echo Grid tutorial; wave-front gating on midpoint: (Position + nn_pos)*0.5 = mid; Length(mid - (0,0,1)) < Wave_Radius → conn_active; gating on midpoint prevents straddling-edge flicker at wave boundary; GeometryNodeForeachGeometryElementInput domain='POINT': fe_in.outputs[1] = Element Index (0-based iteration counter); inside zone, read per-element attributes via SampleIndex(geometry=pre_zone_pts, index=Element_Index) — not via NamedAttribute directly (which returns the full field, not a scalar for the current element); GeometryNodeMeshLine mode='END_POINTS': inputs["Start Location"]=pos_i, inputs["End Location"]=nn_pos_i, inputs["Count"]=2 → 2-vertex 1-edge mesh; accumulate in For Each Output → N disconnected edges; post-zone: MeshToCurve → CurveToMesh (circle profile radius=TUBE_RADIUS, fill_caps=True) → tube mesh; colour_t attribute (FLOAT POINT) must be stored on MeshLine vertices INSIDE the For Each zone to propagate through MeshToCurve → CurveToMesh; outside sources: njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-06 | blender-tutorial-gn-scale-instances-spine-growth | GN Scale Instances — Noise-Driven Organic Spine Growth on a Sphere | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-scale-instances-spine-growth/ | GeometryNodeScaleInstances: inputs[0]=Instances(Geometry), inputs[1]=Selection(BOOLEAN), inputs[2]=Scale(VECTOR field), inputs[3]=Center(VECTOR field), inputs[4]=Local Space(BOOLEAN); outputs[0]=Instances(Geometry); key distinction from Transform Instances: TI applies one uniform delta to ALL instances; ScaleInstances applies an INDEPENDENT field value per instance — evaluated once per instance in the field graph, no For Each zone required; Local Space=True maps scale to the instance's own coordinate axes: essential when instances are tilted (e.g. surface-normal aligned cones), because Local Space=False applies world-axis scale and shears any non-vertical instance; Center=(0,0,0) LOCAL = instance base vertex = growth radiates from attachment point; Center=(0,0,SPINE_LENGTH/2) = midpoint pivot = base retracts AND tip extends simultaneously; three-layer scale field: noise_scale × lat_mult × Grow_Factor; ShaderNodeTexNoise noise_dimensions='3D' available in GN graph as field node — evaluates at InputPosition per vertex without UV; SMOOTHSTEP interpolation_type on MapRange avoids hard noise-patch boundary transitions; IcoSphere subdivisions=4 (642 verts) over UVSphere; lat compression MapRange: |Z| (0→1) → (1.0→0.42) — equator full height, poles compressed; Grow_Factor Group Input (0→1) = animation gate; "Input_2" socket identifier for keyframing via mod.keyframe_insert; Realize Instances node before GroupOutput ensures glTF exporter receives pure mesh geometry; outside sources: Blender Manual Scale Instances Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-06 | blender-tutorial-gn-uv-unwrap-pack-islands-glb | GN UV Unwrap + Pack Islands — Procedural UV for WebXR GLB Export | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-uv-unwrap-pack-islands-glb/ | GeometryNodeUVUnwrap method='ANGLE_BASED' or 'MINIMUM_STRETCH': FIELD NODE — no Geometry socket; evaluates in CORNER domain from the mesh context provided by whichever geometry-socket node consumes the output (StoreNamedAttribute); inputs[0]=Selection(BOOLEAN face), inputs[1]=Seam(BOOLEAN edge), inputs[2]=Margin(FLOAT); output=UV(FLOAT2 CORNER); ANGLE_BASED minimises angular distortion → correct for organic surfaces; MINIMUM_STRETCH minimises area distortion → correct for flat panels; GeometryNodePackUVIslands: also FIELD NODE; inputs[0]=UV(FLOAT2 CORNER), inputs[1]=Selection(BOOLEAN face), inputs[2]=Margin(FLOAT), inputs[3]=Rotate(BOOLEAN, allow island rotation for better bin-packing); output=UV(FLOAT2 CORNER); without packing, islands from UVUnwrap may overlap or scatter outside [0,1] square; Rotate=True typically improves utilisation 10–20%; auto-seam pattern: GeometryNodeEdgeAngle.outputs['Unsigned Angle'] → GREATER_THAN(threshold=radians(SEAM_ANGLE_DEG)) → UVUnwrap.inputs['Seam']; EdgeAngle is EDGE domain field; unsigned angle = dihedral between adjacent face normals (0=coplanar, π=back-to-back); threshold 35–50° stable for organic noise-displaced geometry; below 30° → many tiny islands, poor packing; above 65° → large distorted islands; StoreNamedAttribute domain='CORNER' data_type='FLOAT2' name='UVMap': CORNER domain = one UV per face corner (loop index); vertex shared by 4 faces can have 4 distinct UVs → enables UV seams; FLOAT2 = two-component [U,V], not FLOAT_VECTOR (3D XYZ); name='UVMap' triggers glTF-Blender-IO to write TEXCOORD_0 accessor; 'UVMap.001' → TEXCOORD_1; export_apply=True mandatory — without it GN modifier is skipped, no UV in GLB; export_attributes=True required for Named Attributes (vs standard mesh UV layers); ShaderNodeUVMap.uv_map='UVMap' in material reads the CORNER attribute; SeparateXYZ.X = U coordinate (0→1) drives ColorRamp gradient; outside sources: UV Unwrap Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) + glTF 2.0 Specification §Meshes Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-06 | blender-tutorial-gn-separate-geometry-exploded-view | GN Separate Geometry — Procedural Dissection and Exploded-View Animation | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-separate-geometry-exploded-view/ | GeometryNodeSeparateGeometry: inputs[0]=Geometry, inputs[1]=Selection(BOOLEAN field); outputs[0]="Selection"(matched elements), outputs[1]="Inverted"(complement); CRITICAL distinction from GeometryNodeDeleteGeometry: Delete is destructive and single-output; Separate keeps both halves live and dual-output — both can be wired into independent sub-chains; domain='FACE' evaluates the boolean field at each face centre — correct for planar cross-sections where equatorial faces straddle the split Z; domain='POINT' evaluates at vertices — equatorial ring faces that span Z=0 get ambiguous evaluation (face selected if ANY vertex selected), producing ragged cuts; domain='EDGE' similarly ambiguous; Compare(GREATER_THAN, B=1e-4) on SeparateXYZ(InputPosition).Z avoids equatorial vertices evaluating exactly to False at Z=0; GeometryNodeBoundingBox: inputs[0]=Geometry; outputs[0]=BoundingBox(mesh), outputs[1]=Min(VECTOR constant), outputs[2]=Max(VECTOR constant); Min/Max are NOT mesh — they are Vector fields re-evaluated each frame containing AABB corners; BoundingBox.Max.Z of the upper shell = sphere radius ≈ 1.0 for unit sphere → multiplied by Explode_Gap socket → adaptive gap that scales with any sphere radius; CRITICAL: BoundingBox must be upstream of TransformGeometry — reading BoundingBox after the lift includes the offset in Max.Z, causing exponential gap growth; GeometryNodeTransformGeometry: inputs[0]=Geometry, inputs[1]=Translation(VECTOR), inputs[2]=Rotation(EULER), inputs[3]=Scale(VECTOR); only Translation used here; CombineXYZ packs (0, 0, BBox.Max.Z × Gap) into the Translation vector; GeometryNodeSetMaterial: assigns material data-block reference to all faces of input geometry; wiring: SeparateGeometry.outputs["Selection"]→SetMaterial(shell_amber), SeparateGeometry.outputs["Inverted"]→SetMaterial(core_teal); gap_sock.identifier (e.g. "Socket_2") used for mod.keyframe_insert(data_path=f'["{gap_id}"]'); BoundingBox wire taps BEFORE SetMaterial — material has no effect on AABB; JoinGeometry receives transformed upper + untransformed lower; export_apply=True materialises the GN stack at EXPORT_FRAME=30 (mid-dissection) for the GLB snapshot; outside sources: Separate Geometry Node Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Foundation) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-07 | blender-tutorial-python-bpy-bmesh-dodecahedron | bpy + bmesh — Procedural Dodecahedron via Python Script (Blender 5.1) | blends/scripting/python-bpy-bmesh-dodecahedron/ | bmesh.new(): in-memory mesh buffer with no Blender data-block and zero context dependency — construction works in Text Editor, headless batch (blender --background --python), and add-on operators equally; bm.verts.new(Vector(v)*factor): appends vertex to the bmesh; factor=SCALE/RAW_CIRCUM normalises the golden-ratio circumsphere (radius √3) to SCALE Blender units at vertex-creation time, so no residual scale on the object transform; bm.verts.ensure_lookup_table(): REQUIRED before random-access by integer index — bmesh rebuilds its internal index pool only on demand; missing this call raises IndexError; bm.faces.new([bm_verts[i] for i in face_indices]): creates one pentagonal face from an ordered list of 5 BMVert references; bmesh infers all 30 edges automatically from the 12 face adjacency calls — no explicit bm.edges.new() required; bm.normal_update(): recalculates all face and vertex normals pointing outward from the centroid — bmesh initialises all normals to zero; bm.to_mesh(mesh) + bm.free(): transfers bmesh buffer to a Blender Mesh data-block then frees the in-memory buffer — forgetting bm.free() leaks memory in batch scripts creating many objects; poly.use_smooth=False on mesh.polygons: flat-shading without operator — equivalent to bpy.ops.object.shade_flat() but context-independent; bpy.data.materials.new() + mat.use_nodes=True: creates Principled BSDF node tree via data API; mat.node_tree.nodes.get('Principled BSDF') retrieves by label (locale-dependent) — for locale safety filter by node.type=='BSDF_PRINCIPLED'; bpy.data.objects.new(name, mesh) + collection.objects.link(obj): adds object to scene without any operator or viewport context; keyframe_insert(data_path='rotation_euler',frame=N): inserts a keyed pose directly; kp.interpolation='LINEAR' removes ease-in/ease-out for constant-velocity spin; bpy.ops.export_scene.gltf(): the one unavoidable operator — file-handler ops do not require 3D viewport context; export_apply=True bakes GN modifier and transforms into vertex positions; export_yup=True converts Blender +Z-up to glTF +Y-up; Draco level 6 + WEBP images = Holoflow studio export defaults; V=20 E=30 F=12, Euler characteristic V−E+F=2; golden ratio PHI=(1+√5)/2, R=1/PHI; three vertex orbits: cube corners (±1,±1,±1) × 8, (0,±R,±PHI) even permutations × 12; circumsphere radius √3; outside sources: Blender Python API bmesh module CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-07 | blender-tutorial-shader-procedural-worn-metal-edge-wear | Shader: Procedural Worn Metal — Bevel-Shader Edge Wear, Noise Bump & Roughness Gradient | blends/shading/shader-procedural-worn-metal-edge-wear/ | ShaderNodeBevel: EEVEE-exclusive screen-space curvature detector; inputs['Samples'](INT default 4, production 16), inputs['Radius'](FLOAT world-space metres); outputs['Normal'](VECTOR perturbed); at sharp edges the depth-buffer taps on either side sample divergent face planes → returned Normal tilts toward adjacent face; on flat faces all taps agree → Normal matches surface Normal; has NO effect in Cycles (returns unperturbed surface normal — node evaluates in EEVEE deferred G-buffer pass only); DOT PRODUCT extraction: VectorMath.operation='DOT_PRODUCT' with Bevel.Normal and Geometry.Normal → outputs['Value'](float) = cos(angle between them) = 1.0 on flat, ~0.0 at right-angle edge; Math SUBTRACT(1.0, dot) inverts to edge-wear mask 0.0→flat, 1.0→edge; ShaderNodeValToRGB as wear threshold shaper: elements[0].position=0.28 = wear onset, elements[1].position=0.65 = full wear; interpolation='LINEAR' for gradual oxidation, 'CONSTANT' for hard anodising chip, 'B_SPLINE' for soft rubber wear; ShaderNodeTexNoise for random flat-surface patches: noise_dimensions='3D', Scale=12, Detail=6, Roughness=0.55, Distortion=0.3; Object texture coords (n_tc.outputs['Object']) → scale-invariant, survives apply-transforms, does not depend on UV seams; ColourRamp(B_SPLINE) shapes patch softness; Math MAX combining edge + noise: MAX(a,b)=(a+b+|a-b|)/2 — each channel triggers wear independently without compounding at overlap zones (ADD would over-dirty edges that fall inside high-noise patches); ShaderNodeBump for micro-scratch normals: inputs['Strength']=0.38, inputs['Distance']=0.003 (3mm), Height=Noise.Fac → perturbs shading normal without modifying geometry; wired into Worn PBSDF Normal only, so bump fades with wear mask; ShaderNodeMixShader: inputs['Fac']=MAX.Value, inputs[1]=Clean PBSDF (Metallic=1.0, Roughness=0.06, IOR=2.5 steel approx), inputs[2]=Worn PBSDF (Metallic=0.75 oxide-layer, Roughness=0.72); CRITICAL GLB limitation: Bevel node is screen-space only — does NOT export to glTF; GLB carries flat Metallic/Roughness uniform, not per-pixel wear distribution; WebXR bake workflow: switch to Cycles, UV-unwrap mesh, create 2048×2048 image, bpy.ops.object.bake(type='ROUGHNESS'), plug baked texture into PBSDF Roughness, re-export → metallicRoughnessTexture carries full wear; outside sources: Blender Manual Bevel Node CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Foundation) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) |

| 2026-06-08 | blender-tutorial-gn-convex-hull-art-deco-lamp | GN Convex Hull — Procedural Faceted Art-Deco Lamp Shade (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-convex-hull-art-deco-lamp/ | GeometryNodeConvexHull: inputs[0]=Geometry, outputs[0]=Convex Hull(Geometry); no domain selector, no field inputs, no parameters — pure geometry transform; wraps input point positions in the minimal enclosing convex polyhedron; output faces are EXACTLY planar by construction — ideal for flat shading, Draco compression of constant-run normals, and physics collision proxies; WHY hull creates facets: noise displacement produces peaks and concave valleys; hull bridges concavities → each bridged valley becomes a large planar face; face count = number of extreme points, NOT input vertex count — adding subdivisions in smooth regions adds zero hull faces; NOISE_SCALE controls facet density: 2.2 → ~30 hull faces (art-deco lantern), 5.0 → ~70 hull faces (micro-facet); NOISE_DETAIL must be LOW (2.0) — high detail adds fractal octaves that smooth the displacement envelope → hull converges back toward sphere → faceting collapses; centring chain: (Fac−0.5)×2 → [−1,+1] symmetric displacement — without centring all vertices push outward (balloon, not gem); 4D noise W animation: advancing W translates through a DIFFERENT spatial pattern (topology change), not zoom (scale change) — W_END=1.8 covers ~1.8 field cycles for clear morph; VectorMath SCALE inputs['Scale'] socket available by name in Blender 4.0+ (inputs[3] fallback for older); SetShadeSmooth must be inside GN tree (domain='FACE', inputs[2]=False) — object-level shade-flat does not affect GN output mesh; GLB export: flat-normal mesh → NORMAL accessor constant-run = high Draco ratio; KHR_materials_transmission exports Principled BSDF Transmission Weight; Three.js MeshPhysicalMaterial reads transmission automatically in r152+; outside sources: Convex Hull Node Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |
| 2026-06-08 | blender-tutorial-shader-procedural-woven-fabric | Shader: Procedural Woven Fabric — Wave Interlace + Principled BSDF v2 Sheen | blends/shading/shader-procedural-woven-fabric/ | ShaderNodeTexWave wave_type='BANDS': bands_direction='X' (warp threads repeat along X), 'Y' (weft); wave_profile='SIN' (rounded cross-section), 'TRI' (flat-top cotton), 'SAW' (tapered); Scale=THREAD_COUNT places exactly N thread pairs per UV unit; Interlace gate: MODULO(FLOOR(U×N)+FLOOR(V×N), 2) → 0.0 (weft on top) or 1.0 (warp on top) — FLOOR gives integer cell index, ADD sums row+col, MODULO 2 creates checkerboard; WHY Math chain over Checker Texture: shares THREAD_COUNT constant with Wave nodes, guaranteeing alignment at all densities without secondary Scale parameter; Thread height: WARP_H=warp.Fac×GATE, WEFT_H=weft.Fac×(1−GATE), HEIGHT=WARP_H+WEFT_H → suppresses under-thread to 0 height; Bump node Distance=0.004 (4 mm physical thread height); ShaderNodeBsdfPrincipled v2 Sheen lobe: Sheen Weight=0.88, Sheen Roughness=0.28 → Ashikhmin retroreflection approximation, peaks at grazing view angle, characteristic of micro-fibre textiles (linen, cotton, velvet); Sheen Roughness guide: 0.10–0.25=silk, 0.25–0.35=linen, 0.40–0.60=velvet, 0.70+=mohair; Specular IOR Level=0.04 (cloth has minimal specular); GLB export: KHR_materials_sheen written automatically when Sheen Weight>0 → sheenColorFactor + sheenRoughnessFactor; Three.js r152+ MeshPhysicalMaterial reads both via GLTFLoader; Draco level 6 compresses sinusoidal fold geometry efficiently (smooth vertex positions); outside sources: Blender Manual Wave Texture CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Foundation) + KhronosGroup/glTF KHR_materials_sheen Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-09 | blender-tutorial-shader-principled-hair-bsdf-vrm | Shader — Principled Hair BSDF: VRM Anime Hair with R / TT / TRT Lobe Model | blends/shading/shader-principled-hair-bsdf-vrm/ | ShaderNodeBsdfHairPrincipled (separate type from ShaderNodeBsdfPrincipled): implements Chiang 2016 cylindrical strand model; three lobes — R=cuticle reflection (bright specular band), TT=cortex transmission (translucent backlit glow), TRT=Transmission-Reflection-Transmission glint (bright rear rim, requires point-like back light 2-3x key); parametrization='MELANIN' exposes Melanin(0-1: bleached→black), Melanin Redness(0-1: ash→copper), Tint(colour multiplier); VRM trick: Melanin=0 removes all pigment absorption → Tint becomes sole colour; lobe geometry unchanged so highlight placement stays physical; IOR=1.55 = human cortex refractive index; Roughness = longitudinal (along strand, 0.12=silk), Radial Roughness = azimuthal (around strand, 0.30=anime); Coat adds clear coat (silicone wet-look, 0.35 moderate); Offset shifts R lobe highlight: -3°(math.radians(-3))=toward roots=natural cuticle tilt, 0=top of strand, +3°=unnatural tip highlight; Random Color = per-strand melanin variation (<0.05 for anime uniformity); Random Roughness = per-strand roughness scatter; CYCLES ONLY — returns black in Eevee; no glTF equivalent — Principled Hair BSDF maps to plain opaque in GLB; WebXR path = hair-card bake: Cycles diffuse bake to transparent PNG atlas → atlas on ribbon planes (hair cards) → standard KHR_materials_unlit GLB; back light must be brighter than key to reveal TRT glint; outside sources: Principled Hair BSDF Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + pbrt-v4 Apache-2.0 (Pharr/Jakob/Humphreys, reference lobe implementation) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-09 | blender-tutorial-gn-simulation-zone-reaction-diffusion-turing | GN Simulation Zone — Grey-Scott Reaction-Diffusion: Turing Pattern Emergence | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-simulation-zone-reaction-diffusion-turing/ | GeometryNodeSimulationInput / GeometryNodeSimulationOutput auto-pair in one node group; the Simulation Zone caches geometry+attributes between frames — pre-zone init nodes run every evaluation but the zone only consumes them at frame 0 (or after cache clear); on frames 1+ sim_in reads its own cached geometry, ignoring the pre-zone path; GeometryNodeBlurAttribute iterations=1: computes first-ring topology-weighted average = (Σneighbours)/degree; lap_u = blur_u − u ≈ (Σneighbours − degree·u)/degree — discrete Laplacian proxy; stability criterion for explicit Euler: Du × max(|eigenvalue of (blur−I)|) × DT ≤ 1; on a 4-regular grid max eigenvalue = 2, so Du × 2 × DT ≤ 1 → Du ≤ 0.5 for DT=1/frame; Du=0.20 gives product 0.40 — stable at any GRID_RES; Grey-Scott reaction: dU = Du×lapU − UV² + feed×(1−U); dV = Dv×lapV + UV² − (feed+kill)×V; nonlinear term UV² = autocatalytic coupling driving Turing instability; feed+kill balance determines which Pearson regime: k=0.056 solitons, k=0.060 coral/labyrinth, k=0.062 spots, k=0.065 disappearing spots; NamedAttribute('conc_u') inside sim zone reads from the geometry on the main flow path (sim_in→…→sim_out), which carries the PREVIOUS frame's stored attributes; GeometryNodeInputNamedAttribute.data_type='FLOAT' required to get scalar Fac output; FunctionNodeCompare data_type='FLOAT' operation='LESS_THAN' outputs BOOLEAN; ShaderNodeMath MULTIPLY coerces BOOLEAN to FLOAT 0/1 in Blender 5.1 — Boolean socket auto-coercion to Float is documented behaviour; ShaderNodeAttribute(attribute_type='GEOMETRY', attribute_name='conc_v').outputs['Fac'] reads float named attribute from mesh in material — stable since Blender 4.0+, works in both EEVEE and Cycles; sequential frame_set(f) loop required before GLB export — Simulation Zone evaluates lazily; frame_set(BAKE_FRAME) alone skips intermediate state and exports frame-0 geometry; export_apply=True + export_attributes=True both required — apply bakes GN modifier, attributes writes conc_u/conc_v custom accessors; outside sources: Simulation Zone Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + pmneila/jsexp MIT (Patrick Mineault) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-09 | blender-tutorial-gn-mesh-topology-vertex-valence-heat-map | GN Mesh Topology — Vertex Valence Heat Map: Pole Detection and Topology Diagnostics (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-mesh-topology-vertex-valence-heat-map/ | GeometryNodeEdgesOfVertex: inputs[0]='Vertex Index'(INT field, default=InputIndex — evaluate at each vertex), inputs[1]='Sort Index'(INT, which adjacent edge to return — unused here); outputs[0]='Edge Index'(INT), outputs[1]='Total'(INT = number of adjacent edges = VALENCE); key semantic: Total is a scalar field — connect InputIndex → Vertex Index and Total evaluates to the valence at each vertex without any For Each zone; INT→FLOAT implicit cast on connection to ShaderNodeMapRange inputs[0]; MapRange.data_type='FLOAT', clamp=True; 5-stop ColorRamp: 0.0(blue,val3 N-pole), 0.2(green,val4 ideal quad), 0.4(yellow,val5 E-pole), 0.7(orange,val6), 1.0(red,val8 UV sphere cap); FLOAT_COLOR vs BYTE_COLOR: FLOAT_COLOR = 32-bit/channel, preserves smooth gradient; BYTE_COLOR = 8-bit/channel introduces visible banding at 0.01 red-channel delta between blue/green stops; StoreNamedAttribute domain='POINT' data_type='FLOAT_COLOR': one RGBA colour per vertex; WHY POINT not FACE: EdgesOfVertex queries vertex topology — evaluating at face centres would give wrong valence (face centres average position, not vertex adjacency); ShaderNodeAttribute attribute_type='GEOMETRY' reads per-vertex named attribute in material; WHY Emission not Principled BSDF: diagnostic colour must be lighting-independent — PBSDF darkens cool-coloured vertices on shadow faces, making blue/green indistinguishable; UV sphere topology: cap vertices valence = UV_U (longitude segments), all ring vertices valence = 4; IcoSphere topology: all 12 original vertices valence = 5 (E-poles); after 1 subdivision: original vertices retain 5, new edge-midpoint vertices get 4; practical diagnosis: IcoSpheres preferred for character head meshes because E-poles (valence 5) are distributed uniformly vs UV sphere's 2 high-valence caps; GLB: export_colors=True + export_attributes=True; valence_col accessor = glTF custom attribute (prefixed _valence_col per spec); Three.js: geo.attributes.color or geo.attributes['_valence_col'] depending on whether exporter promotes to COLOR_0; macro: tools/blender-addon/holoflow_macros/gn_mesh_topology_vertex_valence_heat_map.py apply_valence_heat_map(obj); outside sources: Edges of Vertex Node Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-09 | blender-tutorial-gn-accumulate-field-spiral-staircase | GN Accumulate Field — Procedural Spiral Staircase: Prefix-Sum Helical Scaffold (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-accumulate-field-spiral-staircase/ | GeometryNodeAccumulateField: pure field node (no Geometry I/O), prefix-sum over a domain; data_type='FLOAT'/'INT'/'FLOAT_VECTOR'; domain='POINT'/'EDGE'/'FACE'/'CORNER'; inputs[0]=Value(field to accumulate), inputs[1]=Group Index(INT, partitions accumulation — all elements in same group share one running total); outputs[0]=Leading(sum of elements BEFORE current — exclusive prefix sum; Leading[0]=0, Leading[i]=sum(values[0..i-1])), outputs[1]=Trailing(sum INCLUDING current; Trailing[i]=sum(values[0..i])), outputs[2]=Total(sum of ALL elements in group — same value for all elements); WHY Leading not Trailing for step positions: Leading[0]=0 places step 0 at floor level; Trailing[0]=RISE would float step 0 above the floor; WHY FLOAT not INT: INT truncates 0.15→0 for early indices, all first RISE/TRUNCATION steps collapse to floor; MeshLine(count=STEPS, offset=(0,0,0)): all vertices at origin — AccumulateField positions them; CombineXYZ packs scalar Leading into Z component of Vector for SetPosition (Position socket requires VECTOR, direct FLOAT connection causes type error); RotateInstances local=False (world-space Z rotation sweeps tread around post axis); local=True spins tread about its own slab centre — wrong; ObjectInfo transform_space='ORIGINAL' preserves baked Y offset in tread mesh; 'RELATIVE' zeroes offset relative to scaffold origin; bmesh vertex offset baked into mesh before instancing: object.location offset ignored by InstanceOnPoints, must be in mesh vertex data; RealizeInstances before export: Draco compresses realised vertex buffer; without it Draco only compresses prototype tread mesh (16 verts), not 16×16=256 realised verts; export_apply=True required to bake NODES modifier — without it exporter writes empty scaffold mesh; Group Index partitioning: set to per-element field (island, storey number) for independent accumulation sequences in one pass; macro: tools/blender-addon/holoflow_macros/gn_accumulate_field_spiral_staircase.py build_spiral_staircase(obj, steps, rise, twist_deg, tread_w, tread_d, inner_r); outside sources: Accumulate Field Node Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Documentation Team) + blender-python-scripts MIT (Zach Noblitt) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |

| 2026-06-09 | blender-tutorial-gn-rotate-instances-phyllotaxis-sunflower | GN Rotate Instances — Phyllotaxis Sunflower Array: Golden Angle Disc Packing (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-rotate-instances-phyllotaxis-sunflower/ | GeometryNodeRotateInstances: inputs[0]=Instances, inputs[1]=Selection, inputs[2]=Rotation(VECTOR field, Euler XYZ in radians), inputs[3]=Pivot Point(VECTOR, default=(0,0,0)), inputs[4]=Local Space(BOOLEAN, default=True); Local Space=False: rotation applied in WORLD frame — world Z rotation maps instance +X axis to (cos θ, sin θ, 0), achieving radial outward alignment when θ is the placement angle; Local Space=True: rotation applied around the instanceʼs own geometric centre in its local frame — instance spins in place, does NOT sweep radially; phyllotaxis placement: position n-th element at polar(sqrt(n)·c, n·α) where α=2π(2−φ)=GOLDEN_ANGLE_RAD≈2.399963 rad≈137.508°; WHY golden angle not any other angle: any rational p/q approximation causes angular steps to repeat after q seeds, creating q radial spokes; continued-fraction expansion of 2π/α=[1;1,1,1,…] (all ones) = maximally irrational = maximum anti-alignment between successive seeds; sqrt(n) radial spacing gives uniform area density — equal ring area per seed regardless of ring radius; GN field chain: GeometryNodeInputIndex(INT)→ShaderNodeMath MULTIPLY(GOLDEN_ANGLE_RAD)→θ; Index→POWER(0.5)→MULTIPLY(SPACING)→r; θ→COSINE→MULTIPLY(r)→px; θ→SINE→MULTIPLY(r)→py; CombineXYZ(px,py,0)→SetPosition; CombineXYZ(0,0,θ)→RotateInstances.Rotation; seed mesh elongated along +X so Z rotation by θ aligns long axis to radial direction; GeometryNodePoints(COUNT, Radius=0.001): near-zero Radius prevents point cloud self-offset before SetPosition overwrites positions; 144 seeds (Fibonacci)→89 clockwise + 55 counter-clockwise spirals; RealizeInstances before GLB: Draco compresses 144 realised meshes vs single prototype; reusable macro: tools/blender-addon/holoflow_macros/phyllotaxis_scatter.py build_phyllotaxis_tree(instance_obj, count, spacing, material); outside sources: Rotate Instances Node Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Foundation) + Vogel 1979 public domain (Mathematical Biosciences 44:179–189) + blender-python-scripts MIT (Zach Noblitt) |

| 2026-06-10 | blender-tutorial-gn-string-to-curves-3d-text | GN String to Curves — Procedural 3D Text Generator (Blender 5.1) | blends/geometry-nodes/gn-string-to-curves-3d-text/ | GeometryNodeStringToCurves: inputs String(STRING), Size(FLOAT), Character Spacing(FLOAT), Word Spacing(FLOAT), Line Spacing(FLOAT), Text Box Width(FLOAT), Text Box Height(FLOAT); output "Curve Instances"(GEOMETRY) — CRITICAL: each character is a SEPARATE CURVE INSTANCE, not a flat merged curve; align_x=LEFT/CENTER/RIGHT/JUSTIFY/FLUSH, align_y=TOP_BASELINE/TOP/MIDDLE/BOTTOM_BASELINE/BOTTOM, overflow=OVERFLOW/SCALE_TO_FIT/TRUNCATE, pivot_point=MIDPOINT/TOP_LEFT/TOP_CENTER/TOP_RIGHT/BOTTOM_LEFT/BOTTOM_CENTER/BOTTOM_RIGHT; n_s2c.font=bpy.types.VectorFont data-block; built-in BFont(Apache-2.0): bpy.data.fonts.load("<builtin>") or bpy.data.fonts.get("Bfont Regular"); custom font: bpy.data.fonts.load("/abs/path/font.ttf"); WHY RealizeInstances required before FillCurve: FillCurve iterates over SPLINES of a curve geometry — given an instances container it sees no splines and emits empty mesh; RealizeInstances collapses character instances into one flat curve geometry with real splines; GeometryNodeFillCurve: mode="NGONS" preserves bezier outline fidelity, mode="TRIANGLES" = manifold triangulated mesh (required for 3D-print slicers); inputs Curve(GEOMETRY), outputs Mesh(GEOMETRY); GeometryNodeExtrudeMesh mode="FACES": inputs Mesh(GEOMETRY), Selection(BOOLEAN), Offset(VECTOR), Offset Scale(FLOAT), Individual Faces(BOOLEAN); Individual Faces=True: each polygon extrudes independently → each letter gets its own side walls and back face; Individual Faces=False: all faces treated as one slab, outer boundary only — leaves inter-character gaps hollow and joins adjacent letters; Offset=(0,0,depth) via ShaderNodeCombineXYZ with Z=GroupInput.Depth — constrains extrusion to +Z without face-normal field; outputs Mesh(GEOMETRY), Top(BOOLEAN selection of extruded caps), Side(BOOLEAN selection of side walls); GeometryNodeBevelMesh mode="EDGES": inputs Mesh, Selection, Amount(FLOAT), Segments(INT), Profile(FLOAT); Amount=0.008m(~5 0.000000e+00m-height)=narrow specular highlight chamfer; Segments=2=45° fillet approximation (3+ segs for round, higher vertex cost); mode="VERTICES" bevels corners rather than edges — wrong for letter extrusion; STRING ANIMATION LIMITATION: String socket type has NO F-curve interpolation mode — cannot keyframe string values in timeline; workaround A: custom String property on object → Python driver → GN group input socket; workaround B: Python frame loop: mod["Socket_0"]=f"LABEL {i}" then bpy.ops.wm.save_as_mainfile for each frame; GLB export: export_apply=True mandatory to bake NODES modifier before export (without it exporter writes empty carrier mesh); export_animations=False for static text GLB (camera arc is Blender-side only); Draco level 6 compresses the solid letter vertex buffer efficiently; font UNICODE limitation: built-in BFont covers ASCII + basic Latin only; non-Latin scripts require a Unicode-complete .ttf (e.g. Noto fonts, OFL); outside sources: String to Curves Node Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Foundation) https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/text/string_to_curves.html; Inter Typeface OFL 1.1 Rasmus Andersson https://github.com/rsms/inter; Google Fonts Apache-2.0/OFL https://github.com/google/fonts |

| 2026-06-10 | blender-tutorial-compositor-cryptomatte-multilayer-exr | Compositor Nodes — Cryptomatte Masking + Multi-Layer EXR (Blender 5.1) | blends/compositing/compositor-cryptomatte-multilayer-exr/ | CompositorNodeRLayers: outputs include 'Image'(Combined), 'DiffDir'(Diffuse Direct), 'GlossDir'(Glossy Direct), 'Shadow', 'Normal', 'Depth', 'Denoising Normal', 'Denoising Albedo', 'CryptoObject00..02' — all pass sockets only exist after enabling corresponding ticks in View Layer > Passes; CompositorNodeDenoise: inputs['Image'](Combined), inputs['Normal'](Denoising Normal), inputs['Albedo'](Denoising Albedo); use_hdr=True preserves float values above 1.0 — critical for glassy/metallic highlights; OIDN (Intel Open Image Denoise, Apache-2.0) uses a trained CNN on RGB+Normal+Albedo buffers jointly; NLM fallback is spatial-only and smears rather than removes fireflies at low sample counts; scene.cycles.denoising_input_passes='RGB_ALBEDO_NORMAL' required to output the two auxiliary buffers; CompositorNodeCryptomatte: source='RENDER' reads hashes from current render session (vs 'IMAGE' for a pre-rendered EXR); layer_name='CryptoObject00' must match the pass name in the EXR; matte_id: double-quoted object name = exact hash match: node.matte_id='"object_name"'; space-separated for multiple objects: '"a" "b"'; hash is deterministic on object name — rename breaks existing mattes silently; Matte output = float greyscale mask (1.0 = fully selected, 0.0 = not selected); view_layer.use_pass_cryptomatte_accurate=True adds sub-pixel transparent-object precision at ~2x Cryptomatte pass cost; pass_cryptomatte_depth=3 = 3 layers = 6 EXR channels (CryptoObject00..02 RGB+A); CompositorNodeGlare glare_type options: 'FOG_GLOW'=omnidirectional diffuse bloom (correct for gems/glass); 'SIMPLE_STAR'=cross-hatch; 'STREAKS'=anamorphic lens; 'GHOSTS'=lens flare; size=7 → bloom radius ~128px at 1080p (2^size pixel scale); threshold=0.8 = only pixels with luminance>0.8 contribute; mix=0.70 = blend 70 0low onto original; apply Glare only to Cryptomatte-extracted gem pixels then ADD back to avoid full-frame bloom spill; CompositorNodeEllipseMask: width/height=0.0–1.0 fraction of frame; hard edges without blur; CompositorNodeBlur filter_type='GAUSS', size_x/size_y in pixels; MixRGB blend_type='MULTIPLY' for gem extraction and vignette application; Math operation='SUBTRACT' with inputs[0]=1.0 for mask inversion; operation='MULTIPLY_ADD' with inputs[1]=strength, inputs[2]=1-strength normalises vignette range to [(1-s),1]; CompositorNodeOutputFile: format.file_format='OPEN_EXR_MULTILAYER'; format.exr_codec='DWAA'(lossy wavelet, 3-8x vs raw) or 'ZIP'(lossless, 2x); format.color_depth='32'(full float) or '16'(half float, sufficient for grade); file_slots.clear() + file_slots.new(name) before adding links; link must use slot name matching file_slots entry; scene.use_nodes=True enables compositor; scene.render.use_compositing=True runs compositor on render; Denoising passes only available when scene.cycles.use_denoising=True; outside sources: Cryptomatte Specification v1.2.1 BSD-2-Clause Psyop Inc. https://github.com/Psyop/Cryptomatte; Blender Compositor Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/compositing/; filmic-blender MIT Troy Sobotka https://github.com/sobotka/filmic-blender |

| 2026-06-10 | blender-tutorial-shader-procedural-wood-grain | Shader: Procedural Wood Grain — Wave-Band Annual Rings, Anisotropic Fibre Sheen & KHR_materials_anisotropy GLB Export (Blender 5.1) | blends/shading/shader-procedural-wood-grain/ | ShaderNodeTexWave wave_type='BANDS' bands_direction='X': generates parallel oscillating bands along X axis; Fac output oscillates 0→1→0 per ring pair (SIN profile) or 0→1→0 triangle (TRI profile); wave_profile='SIN'=smooth ring cross-section, 'TRI'=flat-top ring (ring-porous hardwood); Distortion parameter=0 here — distortion applied via coordinate perturbation BEFORE Wave node; pre-scale Vector by (RING_FREQ, RING_FREQ, RING_FREQ×LONG_COMPRESS): LONG_COMPRESS=0.06 compresses Z by factor 17 → transforms spherical ring cross-section into 17:1 ellipse that reads as long grain line on flat plank faces; coordinate perturbation chain: ShaderNodeTexNoise(Scale=0.8, Detail=8, Roughness=0.55)→Color(vec3 smooth field)→VectorMath SCALE by DISTORT_STRENGTH=0.38→VectorMath ADD to pre-scaled coords; WHY Color not Fac for perturbation: Color is full 3D vector bending grain lines in all axes; WHY coordinate injection not output mixing: preserves full dark→light→dark ring colour cycle vs averaging to uniform mid-tone; four-stop B_SPLINE ColorRamp: 0.0=dark latewood (0.079,0.033,0.007), 0.42=light earlywood (0.570,0.280,0.072), 0.70=mid-tone (0.330,0.145,0.030), 1.0=dark latewood; B_SPLINE interpolation=gradual density shift = correct ring boundary appearance; fine fibre roughness: ShaderNodeTexNoise(Scale=55, Detail=6, Roughness=0.80)→ShaderNodeMapRange [0,1]→[BASE-VAR, BASE+VAR]=[0.16,0.44]→PBSDF Roughness; ShaderNodeTangent direction_type='UV_MAP': reads U-axis tangent from UV map derivatives; Cube Projection UV has U direction aligned with plank long face (X axis = grain direction) → anisotropic highlight sweeps parallel to grain; Principled BSDF v2: Anisotropic=0.68 elongates highlight into band along Tangent direction; IOR=1.55 (wood range 1.45-1.60); GLB KHR_materials_anisotropy: bpy.ops.export_scene.gltf() writes anisotropyStrength + anisotropyRotation automatically when Anisotropic>0 + Tangent connected; Three.js r153+ MeshPhysicalMaterial.anisotropy reads both values; CRITICAL LIMITATION: Wave Texture evaluates only inside Blender shader engine — ring colour pattern does NOT export to GLB; workflow for GLB colour: Cycles bake BaseColor to 2K image, re-export; anisotropy and IOR export natively; mesh: bmesh box plank (1.8×0.04×0.25m) with Cube Projection UV; reusable macro: tools/blender-addon/holoflow_macros/shader_wood_grain.py build_wood_grain_material(obj, **params); outside sources: Wave Texture Node Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Foundation) https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/shader_nodes/textures/wave.html; KhronosGroup/glTF KHR_materials_anisotropy Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF/tree/main/extensions/2.0/Khronos/KHR_materials_anisotropy |

added Blender 3.6, stable 5.1; FunctionNodeFloatToInt rounding_mode='FLOOR': explicit FLOAT→INT cast preventing implicit-cast rounding ambiguity between sub-versions; 4D Noise Texture W dimension as seed-only axis (changes panel layout without spatial shift); GeometryNodeSeparateGeometry domain='EDGE': keeps edges where selection=True, retains connected vertices, drops all faces; output is a valid edge-only mesh suitable for MeshToCurve; GeometryNodeMeshToCurve: converts each connected edge chain to a poly spline; when input is an edge-only mesh, 'Selection' input of MeshToCurve is redundant (all edges are boundary edges); GeometryNodeCurvePrimitiveCircle mode='RADIUS': inputs['Resolution']=CIRCLE_VERTS(4=square,8=round), inputs['Radius']=GROOVE_RADIUS; GeometryNodeCurveToMesh Fill Caps=True: adds flat polygon at each open tube end — avoids dark interior faces at groove endpoints; JoinGeometry multi-input socket: L.new(geom_a, join.inputs[0]) + L.new(geom_b, join.inputs[0]) adds two separate links on the multi-input socket in Blender 5.1; StoreNamedAttribute domain='FACE' data_type='FLOAT_COLOR' for panel_col: face-domain colours replicated per-corner by glTF-Blender-IO exporter (glTF has no face domain, only vertex/corner); FACE domain attributes arrive in Three.js as non-indexed geometry with duplicated vertices at face seams — correct for hard flat-shaded panels; | 2026-06-10 | blender-tutorial-shader-subsurface-scattering-stylised-skin | Shader: Subsurface Scattering — Stylised Skin: Principled BSDF v2 SSS for VRM Anime Characters (Blender 5.1) | blends/shading/shader-subsurface-scattering-stylised-skin/ | Principled BSDF v2 Subsurface cluster: bsdf.subsurface_method='RANDOM_WALK_SKIN' (vs BURLEY vs RANDOM_WALK); inputs['Subsurface Weight']=0→1 blend from pure diffuse to full SSS; inputs['Subsurface Radius']=Vector (R,G,B) = mean free path per wavelength in scene units; inputs['Subsurface Scale']=global multiplier — head modelled at 0.21m needs Scale=0.06 to avoid 17× over-scatter; inputs['Subsurface IOR']=1.4 (biological tissue); inputs['Subsurface Anisotropy']=0 (Henyey-Greenstein g, 0=isotropic); WHY RANDOM_WALK_SKIN beats BURLEY for thin geometry: Burley diffusion assumes optically-infinite slab — fails for pinna cartilage (1-3mm) and nasal septum (2-3mm), outputs grey; RANDOM_WALK_SKIN traces photon paths until mesh exit — thin shells exit early producing warm red transmitted glow (haemoglobin signature); in EEVEE Next 5.1 method enum is accepted but renderer uses screen-space SSS approximation regardless — use Cycles for faithful ear glow; Subsurface Radius R>G>B encodes biology: haemoglobin absorbs 430nm+575nm (blue+green), transmits 700nm (red) → R longest MFP; anime skin uses (1.0,0.42,0.25) vs photorealistic (1.0,0.20,0.10) for warmer less-red scatter; Sheen Weight=0.18 + Sheen Roughness=0.40: models vellus hair + keratinised dead cells → anime velvet bloom on high cheekbones; Sheen lobe is same micro-fibre physics as woven fabric tutorial — bloom perpendicular to main specular lobe; multi-region approach: face mat SSS_WT=0.55 sheen=0.18; ear mat SSS_WT=0.90 (pinna thin → push translucency) no sheen; lip mat SSS_WT=0.75 roughness=0.30; SSS DOES NOT EXPORT TO GLB: glTF 2.0 has no SSS extension; exporter converts to KHR_materials_transmission+KHR_materials_volume from Transmission weight (not Subsurface weight); for WebXR bake SSS to diffuse texture: bpy.ops.object.bake(type='DIFFUSE', pass_filter={'COLOR'}) then export baked image as Base Color; scene.render.engine='CYCLES' required for correct SSS; scene.cycles.samples=64 sufficient for SSS preview (increase to 256 for final); lights: Area key (soft cheek wrap), Point rim behind-and-above (SSS test light), Point fill neutral; bpy.data.materials.new + mat.use_nodes=True + nt.nodes.new('ShaderNodeBsdfPrincipled') workflow; outside sources: Principled BSDF Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/render/shader_nodes/shader/principled.html; blender-scripting MIT Nicolas Janakiev https://github.com/njanakiev/blender-scripting; glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO |

required for Draco GLB export because Draco compresses vertex buffers, not transform lists; without it export_apply=True may write EXT_mesh_gpu_instancing instead of a standard POSITION accessor; node type identifier 'GeometryNodeRealizeInstances'; inputs[0]=Geometry; inputs[1]='Realize Depth'(INT, default 0=fully recursive, controls nested instance depth); output[0]=Geometry; ORDER LAW: CaptureAttribute(domain='INSTANCE') MUST precede RealizeInstances — placing it after causes field to evaluate at POINT domain (per-vertex random) rather than INSTANCE domain (per-instance random); CaptureAttribute(domain='INSTANCE', data_type='FLOAT'): evaluates FunctionNodeRandomValue at INSTANCE domain=one value per instance; cap.inputs[1]=Value(field), cap.outputs[1]=Attribute(field output referencing stored value); after RealizeInstances the INSTANCE domain is destroyed but captured values are propagated to POINT domain (all vertices of each realized instance share the same value); StoreNamedAttribute(domain='POINT', data_type='FLOAT', name='crystal_hue'): store.inputs[2]=Name, store.inputs[3]=Value; connects cap.outputs[1] to store.inputs[3] — field reference passes through Realize; ShaderNodeAttribute(attribute_type='GEOMETRY', attribute_name='crystal_hue').Fac → float scalar; Fac→HueSaturation.Hue converts scalar to full-spectrum colour; WHY Fac not Color: Color output would give (f,f,f,1) grey for a float attribute; glTF export: export_apply=True bakes NODES modifier including Realize; export_attributes=True writes 'crystal_hue' as '_crystal_hue' custom vertex accessor (glTF spec §2.4 underscore prefix); Three.js: geometry.attributes['_crystal_hue'] (Float32BufferAttribute); DistributePointsOnFaces.Rotation output (added Blender 4.0): Euler aligning +Z to face normal — direct connection to InstanceOnPoints.Rotation; FunctionNodeRandomValue Seed socket: use different seeds for scale (7) and hue (31) to avoid correlation; ObjectInfo.transform_space='ORIGINAL' uses prototype mesh in its own local space; macro: tools/blender-addon/holoflow_macros/gn_realize_instances_crystal_grotto.py patch_realize(obj, attribute_name, seed); outside sources: Realize Instances Node Manual CC-BY-SA 4.0 Blender Foundation https://docs.blender.org/manual/en/latest/modeling/geometry_nodes/instances/realize_instances.html; glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 Khronos Group https://github.com/KhronosGroup/glTF-Blender-IO; blender-scripting MIT Nicolas Janakiev https://github.com/njanakiev/blender-scripting |
| 2026-06-10 | blender-tutorial-python-3d-print-mesh-analysis | bpy + bmesh — 3D Print Prep: Manifold Validation, Overhang Analysis & STL Export | blends/scripting/python-3d-print-mesh-analysis/ | edge.is_manifold: each edge must touch exactly 2 faces — 1=boundary(open shell), 3+=T-junction(non-manifold); bm.calc_volume() signed volume invariant: (1/6)Σ(v₀·v₁×v₂) over triangulated faces — positive=outward-consistent-normals(watertight), negative=all-inverted, near-zero=open/mixed/self-intersecting; bmesh.ops.recalc_face_normals(bm,faces=bm.faces) is MANDATORY for revolution meshes: bottom-fan winds [pole,ring[s],ring[s+1]] and top-fan winds [ring[s],ring[s+1],pole] — opposite orientations; without recalc, calc_volume≈0 and overhang analysis is meaningless; overhang_severity=max(0,-face.normal.z): 0=upward-safe, 0.5=vertical-borderline, 1=straight-down-always-needs-support; threshold_cos=cos(radians(45))≈0.707: faces where normal.z<-0.707 are steeper than 45° from horizontal; bm.faces.layers.float.new('overhang_severity') creates a per-face float layer; ShaderNodeAttribute(attribute_name='overhang_severity',attribute_type='GEOMETRY').outputs['Fac'] reads it in EEVEE material; export_attributes=True writes named attribute as custom glTF accessor; bpy.ops.wm.stl_export() Blender 4.2+ unified operator: forward_axis='Y',up_axis='Z' maps Blender→FDM-slicer convention (build plate=XY, Z=build height); ascii_format=False = binary STL, 5× smaller than ASCII; BVHTree.FromBMesh(bm,epsilon=1e-6).overlap(bvh) detects self-intersections beyond adjacency; outside sources: Blender Manual 3D Print Toolbox CC-BY-SA 4.0 (Blender Foundation) + njanakiev/blender-scripting MIT (Nicolas Janakiev) + glTF-Blender-IO Apache-2.0 (Khronos Group) |
